# THE LIVE ARC
*Opened r2376+c54.10 as the dimension-result propagation arc; **restructured r2376+c54.16 (Daryl) into what it
should have been from the start** — a live document that grows, with a lead register that nothing escapes.*

## ⌗⌗ THE FRONTIER AND SYNTHESIS AUDIT (r2376+c54.86) — *and the ninth gate, built because the same failure happened twice in the one place nobody looks*

**⚑ THE QUESTION ASKED (Daryl):** *has P7's frontier section SHRUNK, and has what left it FED THE SYNTHESIS
SECTIONS at weight?* **⇒ It has shrunk in substance and not on the page; what left it reached the synthesis in
two places and not in the third; and the audit turned up a defect of exactly the kind the c54.60 sweep was run
to fix, in the one place that sweep did not look.**

| finding | where | disposal |
|---|---|---|
| ⚠⚠ ***P14's ABSTRACT asserted the identification P14's own section is titled after DENYING*** — "Which three the generations are seated on, and why it is not the hinges" | P14 `\begin{abstract}` | **REWRITTEN c54.86.** *The hinge $S_3$ stated as the within-state index, the generations seated on the turnaround's deck $\mathbb{Z}_3$, and the wall→generation identification withdrawn in the abstract's own voice* |
| ⚠⚠ ***P14's ABSTRACT PREDATED ITS OWN CENTRAL RESULT*** — no monodromy, no $SU(3)$-from-branching, no second quantisation, no flatness | P14 `\begin{abstract}` | **A FOURTH RESULT ADDED c54.86**, with its negative half stated beside it: *the bundle is FLAT — the discrete content of colour and no force* |
| ⚠ **P7 frontier item 5 stated the revision and then, three sentences later, restated the withdrawn reading as current** | P7 `sec:frontiers` | **FIXED c54.86.** *Worse than a missed propagation: correction and error adjacent, so the paragraph contradicted itself* |
| ⚠ **P7 item 5 called a BUILT sector "the framework's largest unbuilt undertaking"** | P7 `sec:frontiers` | **NARROWED c54.86** to what is actually unbuilt: the descent onto a **propagating** spinor sector |
| ⚠ **P7's synthesis named the COMPACT-FACE route to $\su(3)$ only** — the corpus's only route until c54.42 | P7 `sec:unification-scope` | **BOTH ROUTES NOW CARRIED c54.86**, with the difference stated: the compact face is where a continuous algebra with a curvature could sit; the Lorentzian route gives a **flat** bundle — structure, not coupling |
| ⚠ **P14 said "the bundle question is left open ... where this sector stands" AFTER the section answering it** | P14 `sec:chirality` | **DISAMBIGUATED c54.86.** *Two questions were being called one: which module the colour structure acts on (settled — the branching) and what supplies the TOWER's multiplicity (open)* |
| ⌗ **P7 frontier item 7 was resolved in its own title and still listed as a frontier** | P7 `sec:frontiers` | **RETIRED c54.86** out of the enumerate to a record-of-what-closed paragraph; the list opened at seven and stands at **six** |

**⌗⌗ THE PATTERN, AND IT IS ONE PATTERN.** *A result lands in the section that produced it and does not reach the
places that **summarise** — abstracts, frontier lists, synthesis sections.* ***Those are exactly where a reader
goes FIRST and exactly where a working node edits LAST.*** **The c54.60 sweep fixed five bodies and no summaries.**

**⇒ `check_withdrawn.py` — THE NINTH GATE.** *It carries a REGISTRY of withdrawn claims, each a phrase pattern
plus the markers that count as an adjacent correction.* **A paper may QUOTE what the corpus withdrew — it does
so routinely and honestly — but only if a revision marker follows within 900 characters; a bare occurrence
FAILS.** *Verified against the pre-fix tree: `rc=1`, four bare occurrences across P7 and P14. Against the fixed
tree: `rc=0`, with P3's two quotations passing because each carries its correction.*
**⌗ And the standing order it creates: a withdrawal is not finished until its phrase is in that registry.**

## ⌗⌗⌗ THE FOLD (r2376+c54.90, Daryl-directed) — *the open-problems map is pulled into the lead structure, and the register's scope goes from what one node surfaced to what the corpus owes*

**⚑ WHAT HAPPENED.** *`OPEN_PROBLEMS_MAP` carried the corpus's open problems in its own namespace — cluster-local
codes (`A·1`, `C·7`, `E·3`) with a standing warning that they collide with `THE_PLAN`'s lane codes and must
never be matched across documents.* **Twenty-seven live items are now folded into this register as `L-136`
through `L-162`, each carrying its map code as an ALIAS, and the map is annotated inline with the lead IDs.**

**⌗ ONE ID SPACE, ONE BURNDOWN, ONE SUPERSESSION SCAN.** *The map keeps what only it has — the work-CLUSTERS,
the readiness/priority sort, the runway — and becomes a **view** of this register rather than a second source of
truth.* **A new item now enters the register first and appears there as a view of it, never the other way round.**

**⌗⌗ WHY NOW, AND NOT EARLIER: THE MAP NEEDED INFRASTRUCTURE THAT DID NOT EXIST.** *A register of this size is
only workable with the four gates this fork built —* `check_burndown` *(HOT budget, languishing debt, ID-space
integrity)*, `check_grains` *(staleness of the strategic documents)*, `check_supersession` *(open items answered
by banked work)* **and** `check_withdrawn` *(claims the corpus retracted and kept asserting).* **Without them a
list of thirty open items is a list that rots; the map had none of them and had been rotting quietly for
hundreds of revisions.**

⚠ **CLUSTER J IS REGISTERED AT GERM WEIGHT AND STAYS OFF-RUNWAY BY CONSTRUCTION.** *Nine conjugacy-germ items
join the register so they cannot be lost — a `J` lead is a **candidate**, not owed work at the altitude of A–H,
and each row says so.* **Registering something is not promoting it, and the distinction is written into the rows
rather than trusted to memory.**

**⌗ AND THE THREE LEADS THIS NODE OWES ARE NOW `HOT`** — `L-91` *(the flavour-literature read its own split makes
a prediction about)*, `L-110` *(the sector's discharge condition, now one live clause)* and `L-135` *(the
winding/charge sector, the corpus's largest landing debt)* — **because the fold widened the register to
everything the corpus owes, and without the flag the three things actually in hand would be three rows in
thirty.** ⌗ *`check_burndown` immediately reports `L-91` and `L-110` as LANGUISHING — HOT for more than five
revisions — which is correct and is the gate doing its job rather than a defect to suppress.*

**⚑ AND THE FOLD PAID ON ITS FIRST SCAN.** *`check_supersession`, run over the widened register, put newly-folded
leads at the top of its list against receipts they had never been compared with — the strongest being* **`L-142`
(the map's `C·7`, the A/B-generators ↔ ellipse-foci bridge) against `P03_ellipse_foci`**, *whose claim — foci at
$\pm2/\sqrt3$, the Nariai offset, axis ratio $\sqrt3$ — is the ellipse half of that item, banked independently of
the `bseries_C6_C7` computation the item already cites.* **Twenty-seven items that had never been run against
two hundred and forty-two receipts now are, every revision, automatically.**

## ⌗⌗ THE FOLD'S FIRST CATCH (r2376+c54.94) — *two propositions in two papers wearing one name, found by the machine making the conflation a reader could*

**⚑ HOW IT SURFACED.** *The fold put `check_supersession` across every paper's receipts at once, and it matched
`L-136` — the map's `A2`, whose own text reads "by **P11 prop:wall** this is ordinary GR free evolution" —
against **P14's** `prop:wall`.* ⌗ ***THEY ARE DIFFERENT PROPOSITIONS.*** *P11's is the **wall of
inhomogeneity**: "the wall is not a metric singularity of either species … a regular radiative boundary." P14's
is the **throat wall**: "each throat wall binds exactly one normalizable chiral zero-mode."*

**⌗ LATEX NEVER COMPLAINS, WHICH IS WHY IT SURVIVED.** *Each paper compiles alone, so each `\ref` resolves
locally and correctly.* ***THE HAZARD IS ENTIRELY IN PROSE AND IN THE REGISTERS***, *where `prop:wall` reads as
a unique name and is not one — and where a node deciding what work is owed reads exactly those words.*

| collision | papers | disposal |
|---|---|---|
| `prop:wall` | P11 *dynamics* · P14 *matter sector* | **P11's renamed `prop:radiative-wall` c54.94** *(3 occurrences; the cheaper rename, and the more specific object)*, with a note at the proposition naming both walls and saying they must not be conflated |
| `prop:closure` | P12 *algebroid* · P13 *boundary* | **P13's renamed `prop:residue-is-the-bead` c54.94** *(3 occurrences), which is what it actually says* |

**⇒ AND A CHECK, ADDED TO `check_compile`.** *It reports every label defined in more than one paper — there are
twenty — and **FAILS on theorem-class ones** (`prop:` `thm:` `lem:` `cor:` `defn:`), because those are cited by
bare name in prose and in the registers as if unique.* **Generic section names are reported and not failed:
`sec:intro` lives in eleven papers and nobody is confused by it.** *Zero theorem-class collisions remain.*

**⌗⌗ THE POINT WORTH KEEPING: THE FOLD PAID BEFORE IT STRUCK A SINGLE LEAD.** *Widening the register did not
merely give the scan more items to check — it gave the scan a **corpus-wide view**, and the first thing that view
produced was an ambiguity no single-paper check could see.* ***A machine made the conflation a reader could have
made, which is the cheapest possible way to find one.***

## ⌗ THE LAW OF THIS DOCUMENT

$$\textbf{THE ARC DOES NOT CLOSE.}$$

**Its end state is `updating what is owed in the map` — and that state is reached LAST, on purpose.** *Every
investigation throws off leads, danglers, half-answers, puzzles and things noticed in passing.* ***Those go IN
FRONT of the end state, always.*** **A turn that surfaces a lead and does not register it has lost it**, and the
loss is silent — the next context break takes it with no trace that anything was there.

**⌗ THE THREE RULES.**

1. ***HUNGER OVER TIDINESS.*** *Given a choice between advancing toward the end state and adding one more thing
   in front of it, **add the thing.** The end will still be there. **A list finished early is a list that was
   too short.***
2. ***EVERYTHING SURFACED GETS AN ID.*** *A lead noticed in a turn is registered in that turn, with its origin
   and its first move. **Working memory is not storage.***
3. ***A LEAD LEAVES ONLY BY BEING WORKED OR BY DARYL RETIRING IT.*** *It does not leave by being forgotten,
   by looking small, or by the arc reaching its end. **And a lead that turns out to be a closure candidate goes
   through `PROTECTED_OPEN.md`, not around it.***
4. ***A TURN THAT STRIKES NOTHING OWES A REASON.*** *(Adopted r2376+c54.28, Daryl.)* **Registering is free and
   striking is not, so a node left alone will register.** *A turn that adds leads without striking any says
   plainly that it did, and why; and **a lead older than five revisions that is still `HOT` is worked or
   re-triaged before anything newer is begun.***
5. ***`HOT` IS A BUDGET, NOT A LABEL.*** *(Adopted r2376+c54.28, Daryl.)* **No more than a third of the open
   register may carry it.** *At c54.27 it was on 60% and meant nothing. **Re-triage before adding**, and the
   node does that itself rather than waiting to be told.*

**⌗ AND ALL FIVE ARE NOW MECHANICAL.** *`corpus/check_burndown.py` is the third gate — beside `check_receipts`
(over-claiming) and `check_kills` (closure) — and it is the one that polices **the register itself**. It fails
on `HOT` inflation and on a **stale falsifier**, warns on languishing debt, and prints the struck fraction
**so it cannot quietly fall**. ***The failure mode it exists for — work the newest thing, register two more,
repeat — was fully compliant with rules 1 to 3.***

**⌗ WHY THIS DOCUMENT EXISTS IN THIS FORM.** *The arc was built as a step list with a terminus, and step lists
pull a node toward the terminus — the same bias `PROTECTED_OPEN` diagnoses, one level up: **closure reads as
progress**. A step list also has nowhere to put a lead, so leads stayed in working memory and were listed once,
in prose, in a reply that died. **This document is the fix: the spine is what is being worked, the register is
what the work threw off, and the register is the larger of the two by design.***

## ⌗ THE RESULT BEING PROPAGATED, stated once, at its weight

**Within CR**, extended to $D$-dimensional Schwarzschild–de~Sitter with $2M=r_0^{D-3}-r_0^{D-1}$ ($\alpha=1$):

1. **THE COLLAPSE.** P3's family is charted by the sky angle through $r_0=c\sin w$, and the horizon relation
   must linearise there to a **single** multiple-angle — P3: $2/\sqrt3$ is *"the one scale removing the
   residual harmonic."* The two powers are $D{-}3$ and $D{-}1$, so the harmonics below the top number
   **exactly one at $D=4$ and $D=5$, two or more from $D=6$**, against **one** available scale.
   ⇒ **from six dimensions up there is no collapse, no forced fold, and no generation count to read at all.**
2. **THE FOLD IS $D-1$.** $D=4$ → a 3-fold at $c=2/\sqrt3$; $D=5$ → a 4-fold at $c=1$. Via the corpus's own
   `COMBINATORICS_LEDGER` L8.1-a weld (roots ≡ hinges ≡ walls ≡ generations, **one 3, and the object is the
   dial**), the fold **is** the generation count.
3. **THE PARITY.** $2M$ is odd in the signed offset **iff $D$ is even**. At $D=5$ the offset parity
   $R:r_0\mapsto-r_0$ **fixes** each geometry instead of conjugating it ⇒ no mass-reflection $\mathbb{Z}_2$,
   no $\mathbf3\oplus\bar{\mathbf3}$, no outer factor of $\mathrm{Aut}(A_2)$, **and no $\gamma^5$** — whose
   Clifford generator on the cut *is* that parity's reflected leg.
   ⇒ **$D=5$ would carry four generations and NO chirality.**

$$\textbf{So four dimensions is the only one carrying both a generation count and a chirality — and there the count is three.}$$

**It runs both ways**: $4\mathrm{D}\Rightarrow$ three generations, and (a count at all $+$ chirality)
$\Rightarrow4\mathrm{D}$. *The second direction uses no observational input.* **Three generations and four
dimensions are one fact in CR, read at two ends.**

**⌗ ALTITUDE — and it is not negotiable downward or upward.** *Forced **within CR**; coherence, not
correspondence; the same weight as the rest of P14's flavour skeleton.* **It does not prove the world is
four-dimensional.** *It shows CR's flavour skeleton is available at exactly one dimension, and that dimension
is the observed one.* **Every propagated site must carry that altitude**, and any site that states it harder
is a defect this arc created.

**⌗ WHAT IS ASSUMED, to be repeated at every load-bearing site.**
- **(A1)** ~~The $D$-dimensional metric function is the standard **Tangherlini–de~Sitter** one — an extension,
  not a rederivation.~~ ***DISCHARGED r2376+c54.29 (`L-01`).*** *CR's operator is the **kernel of the matter
  functional** (P8 `thm:kernel`), not a metric function; run at general $D$ it gives $rf'+(D-3)(f-1)+2\Lambda
  r^2/(D-2)=0$, whose **entire** solution space is Tangherlini–de Sitter with $M$ the single constant of
  integration.* **What the extension assumed, the construction supplies.** *Two inputs remain and are
  registered: the **lock** at general $D$ (`L-89`) and the **round** transverse space (`L-90`).*
- **(A2)** The single-harmonic **collapse is still a requirement** at other $D$ as it is at four. *(Step 0's T2 tested it against the corpus's **own** operational property — the $D-1$ equally-spaced dial positions carrying one mass and being the roots — and it holds to machine precision at $D=4,5$ and fails at $O(1)$ from $D=6$. **So the criterion is the corpus's, not an import.** It remains an assumption in the sense that a higher-$D$ construction might not **demand** the collapse; nothing tests that.)*
- **(A3)** ~~the gnomonic chart is a 4D import~~ — **DISCHARGED c54.9**: P3's straight-line argument is
  dimension-independent (verified on $S^n$, $n=2\ldots8$), which is the form P3 itself calls forced.

of "colourless"**.* ⌗ **⓶ "the corpus has no particle-level account of what a baryon IS" — FALSE:** *P14 gives one — **second quantisation places a three-fermion state in the exterior cube, one-dimensional and generated by $\varepsilon$, returning* **baryon 1, diquark 0, meson 1** *— "every channel the Standard Model has, with the configuration group SELECTED rather than chosen."*** ⌗ **⓷ AND THE CHECK THE ROW CALLED "NOT CHECKED" IS MADE:** *P14 states it and declines to draw on it —* ***"the representations of $D_6$ that are trivial on the deck $\mathbb{Z}_3$ — which is what carrying no colour would mean — are its FOUR one-dimensional ones, so a colourless sector on this structure has total dimension four."*** ⇒ **And P14's own specified split is "12 coloured against 3 colourless (**4 with a right-handed neutrino**)"** — ***so the structure produces the number the split needs.*** ⌗ *And the objection that would kill it is pre-answered in the same paragraph: every $\mathbb{Z}_2$ grading splits the four $2+2$, **"and it is not a failure, because colour does not see the chirality of a colour singlet in the Standard Model either"** — $SU(3)$ acts trivially on $\nu_L,e_L,e_R,\nu_R$ alike.* ⛔⛔ **AND P14'S RESTRAINT IS NOT OVERRIDDEN, WHICH IS WHY THIS CLOSES NOTHING:** *the four is* ***"a count of GRADINGS and not of FIELDS, and this paper draws nothing from it."*** **A grading count matching a field count is a coincidence until a map is built.** ⇒ ***PO-5 STAYS OPEN and its closure runs by `PROTECTED_OPEN`'s two stated routes.*** *Receipt `C1_the_rows_premises_are_stale_and_the_split_is_specified` (15 checks).* ⛭⛭ **THE LIVE QUESTION ANSWERED IN FORM r2476 — AND THE ANSWER IS IN THE CORPUS'S OWN PRACTICE, because the coloured side already made the crossing.** *r2464 restated the row as **"what would turn a count of GRADINGS into a count of FIELDS"**.* ⌗ **P14 NAMES THE LICENCE IT USED:** *"Second quantisation — the ordinary step, **available because the three wall modes are ONE OPERATOR'S KERNEL and therefore identical particles** — places a three-fermion state in the exterior cube … baryon 1, diquark 0, meson 1."* *And what makes it a **count**: "that count is a **well-defined INDEX** … the leaf is compact and **its Dirac operator carries a well-defined analytical index**."* ⇒⇒ ***A COUNT OF GRADINGS BECOMES A COUNT OF FIELDS WHEN THE GRADINGS ARE THE KERNEL OF AN OPERATOR. Kernel ⇒ identical particles ⇒ second quantisation ⇒ fields — the whole bridge, crossed once.*** ⛭ **AND THE TWO SIDES ARE NOW EXACTLY DISTINGUISHED:** *the **coloured three** are the **index of a Dirac operator on a compact leaf** ⇒ an operator ⇒ **fields**; the **colourless four** are the **one-dimensional representations of $D_6$ trivial on the deck $\mathbb{Z}_3$** ⇒ no operator ⇒ **gradings**.* ⇒ ***So P14's "a count of gradings and not of fields, and this paper draws nothing from it" is EXACTLY RIGHT, and now for a stated reason: the four are a representation-theoretic fact about a GROUP; the three are the kernel of SOMETHING.*** ⛭⛭ **AND IT NAMES WHAT P13's "REMAINS UNBUILT" MEANS:** ***THE UNBUILT THING IS AN OPERATOR, NOT A SECTOR.*** ⇒ **So the row's live question is now buildable and sharply stated:** ***is there an operator on this structure whose kernel is the four colourless gradings?*** *— not "can a colourless sector be built" but* **"can the operator whose index counts four be exhibited."** ⚠ *Nothing here exhibits one or argues one exists;* ***PO-5 stays open and its closure runs by `PROTECTED_OPEN`'s two stated routes.*** ⌗ *Route: **the same move that answered `L-233` — ask what the corpus DID in the case that worked and read the licence it used**, rather than asking what might work in the case that has not. Receipt `C2_gradings_become_fields_by_being_a_kernel` (12 checks).* ⛭⛭⛭ **NARROWED r2525 — PO-5's MISSING OPERATOR AND `L-242`'s UNDEVELOPED IDENTIFICATION ARE ONE GAP.** ⌗ *P14 names the opening as **TWO** discrete structures: **"the orientation parity and the threefold symmetry of the slicing … the one opening through which matter might enter"** — and its delivered result spends the **$S_3$** half.* ⛭ **AND THE CALCULATION IS ON THE MASSLESS SIDE THROUGHOUT:** *the **massless radial Dirac problem**, the **massless Dirac operator's** first-order pair, and P14's own boundary — **"the zero-modes are massless, and their splitting is electroweak physics, EXTERNAL TO THE GEOMETRY"**.* ⇒ *And P6 says what that sector IS: **"the $R$-symmetric sector is exactly the offset-free, massless vacuum"**, with mass **"the $R$-odd DEPARTURE from it"**.* ⇒⇒ ***SO P14 COMPUTES ON $R$'s EVEN SECTOR, AND THE HIGGS MECHANISM — IN CR'S OWN TERMS — IS EXACTLY WHAT TAKES YOU OFF IT. That is not an omission: it is the BOUNDARY OF THE CALCULATION, stated from opposite sides by two papers, neither naming the other's structure.*** ⛭⛭ **AND THAT IS WHY THE FOUR HAVE NO OPERATOR.** *r2476 found the asymmetry — **the coloured three are the INDEX of a Dirac operator** and cross to fields by being a kernel, while **the colourless four are $D_6$ representations trivial on the deck**, a fact about a group.* ⇒ ***AND P14's OPERATOR IS THE MASSLESS $\gamma^5$-GRADED ONE, so any operator whose kernel were the colourless four MUST LIVE OFF THE MASSLESS SECTOR — precisely where P6 puts electroweak breaking.*** ⇒⇒ ***PO-5's "is there an operator whose kernel is the four?" and `L-242`'s "does CR say anything about the Higgs MECHANISM?" ARE ONE QUESTION: both ask what happens when you leave $R$'s even sector.*** ⌗ **SO THE VEIN GAINS A DIRECTION IT DID NOT HAVE:** *the search is **not for any operator** but for an* ***$R$-ODD one, on the sector the corpus has already called electroweak.*** ⚠ *`PO-5` stays open — **nothing here builds an operator** — and no mass is derived: **P14 itself says the splitting is external to the geometry**. Receipt `Q1_the_missing_operator_and_the_higgs_identification_are_one_gap` (13 checks).* ⛭⛭⛭ **AND THE DIRECTION HAS AN EXACT OBSTRUCTION r2526 — THE QUESTION INVERTS.** ⌗ *Why the coloured three are fields, in the algebra: **$\{\gamma^\mu,\gamma^5\}=0$ for every $\mu$** (verified in the chiral basis), so the massless Dirac operator **anticommutes** with $\gamma^5$, $\ker D$ splits into $\pm$ eigenspaces, and $\dim\ker_+-\dim\ker_-$ is well defined.* ***That is the whole machinery by which P14 turns three gradings into three fields.*** ⛔ **AND A MASS TERM DESTROYS IT:** *$[m\mathbb 1,\gamma^5]=0$ — a Dirac mass **commutes** with $\gamma^5$ — so **$\{D+m,\gamma^5\}\ne0$**, while $\{D,\gamma^5\}=0$ without it. **It is the mass, and only the mass, that breaks the grading.*** ⇒⇒ ***THE VERY THING THAT MAKES THE COLOURED THREE FIELDS IS DESTROYED BY GOING $R$-ODD. An $R$-odd operator has no index in P14's sense.*** ⛭⛭ **SO THE QUESTION INVERTS, and that is the result:** *r2525 said **the operator must be $R$-odd**; r2526 says **an $R$-odd operator cannot deliver its four the way the massless one delivers its three**.* ⇒ ***"Is there an operator whose kernel is the four?" was the WRONG QUESTION — not because the answer is no, but because "kernel of a graded operator" is a structure ONLY THE $R$-EVEN SECTOR HAS.*** ⌗ **AND THAT IS WHY P13's "REMAINS UNBUILT" HAS STAYED UNBUILT.** *r2476 read it as **"the unbuilt thing is an OPERATOR, not a SECTOR"**.* ⇒ ***Sharper: the unbuilt thing is a BRIDGE — some way for gradings to become fields that is NOT "be a kernel" — because on the $R$-odd side the kernel route is structurally unavailable.*** ⇒ *And that is `L-242`'s Higgs question with a reason it is hard: **the corpus's one crossing from grading to field runs through a grading that mass breaks**.* ⚠ *Not claimed: that **no bridge exists** — only that the kernel one does not reach there — or that **mass cannot be described**; ordinary QFT does it daily, and the claim is about the INDEX construction. `PO-5` stays open.* ⌗ ***And the narrowing came from Daryl's correction: two revisions earlier this line had recorded the Higgs as a principled decline and closed the turn. The vein's interior moved twice in two revisions because that reading was refused.*** Receipt `Q2_the_kernel_route_is_unavailable_off_the_R_even_sector` (12 checks). | `PROTECTED_OPEN` `PO-5` · `THE_PLAN` standing item ② | **the question is INVERTED r2526**: not "is there an $R$-odd operator?" but ***what BRIDGE from grading to field exists that is not "be a kernel"?*** — the kernel route is structurally unavailable off the $R$-even sector | **OPEN · PROTECTED (`PO-5`)** |
| **L-175** | ⌗⌗ **REGISTERED r2378 by the same fold — `PROTECTED_OPEN`'s **`PO-9`**, also unregistered.** ***The dimensional rise, and what the descent must look like*** — *the object is the **substrate's** dimension, **never the cut's**.* ⛔ ***AND ITS GUARD IS THE LOAD-BEARING PART, so it is carried in the row rather than trusted to memory: the CUT's dimension is settled at four (c54.10); the SUBSTRATE's is bounded BELOW and never above.*** *A node that reads the first as capping the second has re-made the c54.6 error the c54.6 correction exists to prevent — which is why this row is `PROTECTED` and not merely open.* ⌗ *Homes: `STATE_matter_sector` · `algebroid_paper`.* ⛭⛭ **NARROWED r2466 — BOTH HALVES OF THE ROW'S OWN NEXT STEP NOW HAVE AN ANSWER OF THE RIGHT KIND, AND NEITHER IS A CAP.** ⌗ *The chain, in p0's own words: **"the five-dimensional $\mathrm{dS}_5=SO(5,1)/SO(4,1)$ … of which the four-dimensional $\mathrm{dS}_4$ is the BACKGROUND its leaves carry"** — substrate 5, leaf 4, space $S^3$.* ⌗ **⓵ WHAT A DESCENT FROM $D>5$ MUST LOOK LIKE:** *a codimension-one cut of $\mathrm{dS}_D$ gives a $(D{-}1)$-leaf, so* ***$\mathrm{dS}_6$ gives a FIVE-dimensional leaf — a $4{+}1$ spacetime — and needs $D-5$ further reductions.*** ⇒ **And the construction is single-step by design and says so:** *"one slicing plane — a door — swings about one fixed line in the substrate, the hinge, and the whole family of cuts is **the single arc of that swing**."* ***One hinge, one door, one swing.*** ⇒⇒ ***SO A DESCENT FROM $D>5$ MUST BE MULTI-STEP, AND THE CORPUS HAS NO SECOND DOOR: whatever supplied the extra reductions would be NEW STRUCTURE, not a re-reading of what exists.*** ⌗ **⓶ AND WHAT WOULD BOUND THE SUBSTRATE ABOVE: the STEP COUNT does** — *the only candidate the construction supplies.* ⛔ ***AND NOT THE CUT'S DIMENSION, which is exactly this row's guarded error: the bound found here is about the NUMBER OF REDUCTIONS between substrate and leaf, a different quantity from the leaf's dimension.*** ⚠ **AND IT BOUNDS CONDITIONALLY, WHICH IS WHY THIS NARROWS RATHER THAN CLOSES:** *$D=5$ is forced **only if** the descent is one step — and one-step-ness is a **feature of the construction**, not a theorem about substrates.* ⇒ ***Nothing here caps the substrate unconditionally; PO-9's object — "bounded below only" — survives intact, and its closure runs by `PROTECTED_OPEN`'s two stated routes.*** *Receipt `E1_a_descent_from_D_above_five_must_be_multistep` (14 checks).* ⛭⛭ **NARROWED A SECOND TIME r2474 — ONE-STEP-NESS IS NOT A BARE FEATURE; RULE 2 GOVERNS IT.** *r2466 left **"$D=5$ is forced only IF the descent is one step"**, with one-step-ness looking like a construction habit.* ⌗ **IT IS NOT.** *p0 names the criterion: **"least-arbitrariness being the programme's own criterion of necessity (Rule 2 …): a symmetry-breaking modulus is the ADJUSTABLE PARAMETER that criterion rejects."*** *And **P14 already applies it to a COUNT**, one paper over, in an argument nobody had connected to dimension: the least-arbitrary construction places a plane on each of the three hinges, and* ***"a one-hinge truncation is excluded not as disfavoured but as carrying an unfixed arbitrary modulus, which the principle forbids."*** ⇒⇒ ***SO THE CORPUS ALREADY EXCLUDES A STRUCTURE ON THE GROUND THAT ITS SELECTION IS UNFIXED — AND A SECOND SLICING STEP WOULD NEED ITS OWN SELECTION.*** **The same argument that fixes the hinge count governs the step count: not taste, not simplicity, and nothing about the leaf's dimension.** ⛔⛔ **AND THE CONCLUSION IS DECLINED, WHICH IS THE POINT OF THE ROW:** ***a second step's selection might be FORCED BY THE FIRST, carrying no free modulus, in which case Rule 2 does not exclude it — and nothing in the corpus examines that case.*** ⇒ ***THE SUBSTRATE IS NOT BOUNDED ABOVE HERE; PO-9's object survives intact.*** *What the narrowing delivers is that the r2466 condition is **not free-floating**: it is decided by **one sharp question — CAN A SECOND SLICING BE NON-ARBITRARY, ITS SELECTION FORCED BY THE FIRST RATHER THAN CHOSEN?*** ⌗ **AND THE GUARD IS OBEYED RATHER THAN ARGUED AROUND:** *no step uses the cut's dimension; **the argument runs entirely on Rule 2 and a count of MODULI**, which is a different quantity.* ⌗ *Route: asking the row's OWN next question and then **reading the principle at source instead of from a memory of it**. Receipt `E2_one_step_ness_is_governed_by_rule_two` (13 checks).* | `PROTECTED_OPEN` `PO-9` | **the whole row now turns on one question r2474 states**: ***can a second slicing be non-arbitrary — its selection FORCED by the first rather than chosen?*** — and it is a question about the swing, not about dimension | **OPEN · PROTECTED (`PO-9`)** |

they compose asymmetrically — CR supplies what may exist, the Higgs sector how it gets mass, and CR's own theorem says it cannot supply the strengths.** ***Not absorption: something CR's answer leaves room for by construction, and at F3 is constrained by.*** ⌗ *`THE_BASE_RATE`'s discriminant, applied to our own claim: it **DELETES** an apparent conflict and adds no structure — **the favourable side** — **but the favourable side is not a licence and the base rate is one in two**.* ✔✔ **THE NEXT STEP WAS ALREADY DISCHARGED, AND THE ROW NEVER SAID SO — found r2482.** *Its next step read **"state the relation in one place: what CR supplies, what it takes as given, what it forbids"**.* ⇒ ***That document exists: `CR_AND_THE_HIGGS.md`, whose §4 is literally "THE RELATION, STATED"*** *— written at r2414, brought current at r2449, and **the row's next-step field was never updated**.* ⌗ **WHAT IT SAYS:** ***"CR is a SELECTION-RULE theory; ## ⌗ WHY THIS IS NOT AN "END OF THE LINE" ITEM — the connections, named now so they are not lost

- **It connects back to the least-arbitrariness question** that opened this excursion. c54.5 established that
  the criterion **cannot rank dimensions** — $\dS_D$ is moduli-free for every $D$. **This result does not
  contradict that; it comes from somewhere else entirely**: not from the substrate's symmetry but from the
  **matter sector's own content**. *The criterion is silent on $D$; the flavour skeleton is not.* **These are
  two different questions that had been running as one, and separating them is part of the result.**
- **It bears on the colour problem, which is NOT dropped.** The dimension excursion sharpened it rather than
  answering it: the obstruction moved from *"$\su(3)$ cannot act internally at all"* (true at $\dS_5$) to
  *"what reduces the normal structure group $\so(6)\to\su(3)$?"* And this result constrains the shape of any
  answer: **the CUT is four-dimensional**, so a rise puts $\su(3)$ in the normal factor $\so(D-4)$ — which is
  exactly where c54.8 found it can act internally.
- **⚠ AND THE TWO RESULTS MUST NOT BE FUSED.** *The cut's dimension is settled here; **the substrate's is
  not**. Every constraint on the substrate remains a lower bound. A site that reads this result as capping the
  substrate has re-made the c54.5 error the correction at c54.6 exists to prevent.*
